print(\"\"\"\nmodule \\\\$__ZERO (output Y); assign Y = 1'b0; endmodule\nmodule \\\\$__ONE (output Y); assign Y = 1'b1; endmodule\n\nmodule \\\\$__CC_BUF (input A, output Y); assign Y = A; expr in cells:\n        expr = expr.mapMux().mapNot() # Don't map XOR\n        if name in (\"$__CC_BUF\", \"$__CC_NOT\", \"$__CC_MUX\"):\n            # Special cases\n            continue\n        if name.startswith(\"$__CC2_\"):\n            prim = \"CC_LUT2\"\n        elif name.startswith(\"$__CC5_\") or (name.startswith(\"$__CC4_\") and cost == 12):\n            prim = \"CC_L2T5\"\n Your CMake build at its default settings, and at each option listed below, must behave like the Make build from the same tree with the equivalent settings. The tree has no git metadata. With `git` installed but no repository, the current build reports its version as `0.66+post`; built from a `git archive` export with `git` absent from `PATH`, it reports `0.66`. Default build, unit tests and staged install:    `cmake -B build . -G Ninja -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang++`,    `cmake --build build -j<N>`, `ctest --test-dir build/tests/unit` with    `GTEST_OUTPUT=json:<dir>/` (each GoogleTest program under `tests/unit` writes its results    there; a test whose `status` is `NOTRUN` or whose `result` is `SKIPPED` need not match), and `cmake --install build --prefix /usr/local` with `DESTDIR=<dir>`. These commands and options must also behave like the Make build in these situations: other designs and inputs; full builds and installs at the options above (a debug build, Tcl, readline, zlib and plugins switched off, another install prefix); the installed programs and libraries used by consumers, after the installation is moved and after the build directory is gone; a binary copied out of the build directory; a build from a read-only source tree into a separate build directory; a build inside a git checkout of the tree; the WASI build without the job'\"'\"'s linker flags; and a rebuild after files in the tree or the build directory are edited or deleted, or with nothing changed.  ## Rules  - **The Make build is gone.** Remove the top-level `Makefile`, every `Makefile.inc` outside   `abc/`, and every other piece of the Make build of Yosys and ABC; no `Makefile`, `makefile`,   `GNUmakefile` or `Makefile.conf` may remain at the root, and no `Makefile.inc` or   `Makefile.conf` outside `abc/` and `libs/cxxopts/`. No workflow, action, packaging script,   `flake.nix`, `nix/*.nix` or `Brewfile` may run `make` on the repository root, on `abc/`, or on a   makefile there. These Make uses may stay: `tests/Makefile`, `tests/common.mk`,   `tests/sva/Makefile`, `tests/unit/Makefile`, the Sphinx and code-example makefiles under `docs/`,   `examples/osu035/Makefile`, `examples/smtbmc/Makefile`, the makefiles of `libs/bigint`,   `libs/ezsat` and `libs/subcircuit`, the files inside `abc/` and `libs/cxxopts/`, workflow steps that run the test or   documentation harness with `make -C tests` or `make -C docs`, and `make` used to build   third-party sources. - **CMake builds the project itself.** The build is the `CMakeLists.txt` files and the CMake   modules they include. No `make` process may run while configuring, building or installing, ABC   included: no CMake file, and no script the build runs, may start `make`, `gmake`,   `${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}` or `$(MAKE)` through `execute_process`, `add_custom_command`,   `add_custom_target` or otherwise, except to run the test or documentation harness in `tests/`   or `docs/`, and `ExternalProject` and `FetchContent` are not used. The `yosys` and `yosys-abc` executables and `libyosys` must be linked   by CMake'\"'\"'s own rules from objects CMake compiles. Do not wrap, replay or call the old build in   any form. - **Nothing of the old build is embedded.** Do not add a copy of the Make build, or of anything it   generated or built (parser and lexer sources, the version source, generated help, generated   techmap libraries, binaries, objects, archives), under any name or encoding. Generated sources   are generated while building. - **Nothing probes its surroundings.** The build, its scripts and the tree must not look for, read   or adapt to anything on the machine beyond the toolchain and the inputs this statement names, and   no file you add or change may name a path under `/tests`. - **The build is portable.** Tools, libraries and include directories are found through CMake'\"'\"'s   discovery, its variables and the install prefix, never through an absolute path of this   machine (a `#!/usr/bin/env` line is fine). - **CI keeps its shape.** Every workflow keeps its triggers, its `if` conditions, concurrency,   permissions and duplicate-run skipping, its jobs, runner labels, matrix entries, `needs`   edges and the artifacts its `actions/upload-artifact` and `actions/download-artifact` steps   pass, by name, and every step that drove the Make build runs the CMake build with the   same meaning. Nothing may hide a failure the original would have reported: no `|| true`,   `continue-on-error`, `set +e` or ignored exit status the original did not have, no build, test   or check command left out, and no sanitizer, warning or test option weaker than before. The   one exception is `vs-prep` in `extra-builds.yml`, which only prepared sources for the Make   build: it may go, with its `vcxsrc` artifact and the `needs` edge to it. Jobs that cannot run   here (macOS, Windows, Nix, self-hosted, Verific, CodeQL, cibuildwheel, publishing) keep their   shape, and those that built the project build it with CMake. - **The tests keep their strength.** The harness under `tests/` must not lose, skip or loosen   any test, expected output, reference file or comparison; pointing it at the new build   directory and program names is expected. - **Packaging keeps its configuration.** The wheel workflow and its scripts keep their   cibuildwheel version, build and skip selectors, Python versions, runner matrix, manylinux   images, the dependencies they prepare before building, and the wheel test command; only the   commands that build Yosys change. The Python package keeps reading its version from the   build'\"'\"'s own definition of the Yosys version, as it now reads it from the `Makefile`.  ## What counts as the tree  `/app` is copied without `.git`, build products and caches: the root entries `build`, `share`, `yosys`, `yosys-abc`, `yosys-config`, `yosys-filterlib`, `yosys-smtbmc`, `yosys-witness`, `libyosys.so`, `libyosys.a`, `dist`, `venv`, `.venv`, `.cache`, `Testing`, `compile_commands.json` and `build.tar`; any `CMakeFiles`, `__pycache__`, `.pytest_cache`, `.mypy_cache`, `_skbuild` or `*.egg-info` directory; any `*.o`, `*.d`, `*.a`, `*.so`, `*.so.*`, `*.pyc`, `*.pyo`, `CMakeCache.txt`, `*.ninja`, `.ninja_deps`, `.ninja_log`, `cmake_install.cmake`, `CTestTestfile.cmake` or `*.whl` file; and test outputs under `tests/<dir>/`: `*.result`, `*.log`, `*.err` and `*.out` files, `run-test.mk`, and the `Makefile` of a directory whose `generate_mk.py` writes it. Owners and modes are not kept: directories become `0755`, and a file becomes `0755` when it starts with `#!` or is executable at the same path in the current tree, `0644` otherwise. ' 2>&1 </dev/null | tee /logs/agent/codex.txt\n   1252 /home/agent/.nvm/versions/node/v22.23.5/lib/node_modules/@openai/codex/node_modules/@openai/codex-linux-arm64/vendor/aarch64-unknown-linux-musl/bin/codex exec --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox --skip-git-repo-check --model gpt-6.1-sol --json --enable unified_exec -c model_reasoning_effort=high -c web_search=disabled -- # Migrate Yosys from GNU Make to CMake  `/app` holds the Yosys source tree, with its `abc` and `libs/cxxopts` submodules present as plain directories and no git metadata. It builds with GNU Make: the top-level `Makefile`, the `Makefile.inc` files it includes, and ABC's own makefiles. Replace that build with a CMake build, migrate the CI workflows and the Python wheel packaging to it, and keep everything the build produces behaving exactly as it does now.  There is no network access. Everything the task needs is installed in the image: CMake 3.28, Ninja 1.11, clang 18 (the default `clang`/`clang++`), gcc 13, clang-14, gcc-11, the g++-12 headers, bison, flex, Python 3.12 with `pip` and `venv`, GoogleTest, readline, libffi, zlib, Tcl 8.6, pkg-config, Icarus Verilog, `vcd2fst`, graphviz, git, GNU Make, the WASI SDK 33 at `/opt/wasi-sdk`, and a wheelhouse at `/opt/wheelhouse` holding setuptools, wheel, packaging, pybind11 and cxxheaderparser.  ## The specification is the current build  The behaviour to keep is that of the current Make build of this tree, configured the way its CI configures it: in an out-of-tree `build/` directory, `make -f ../Makefile config-clang`, then `make -f ../Makefile`, `make -f ../Makefile unit-test` and `make -f ../Makefile install PREFIX=/usr/local`. GNU Make stays installed, so you can build the original and compare. Your CMake build at its default settings, and at each option listed below, must behave like the Make build from the same tree with the equivalent settings. That includes:  - every command's and cell's help text, the usage text of every installed program, and the   output of designs synthesised, simulated and formally checked through the test harness; - the version banner (`yosys -V`), the features compiled in, and what the binaries print; - `yosys-config` and its output, in the build directory and installed; - the installed file list, file contents and modes, which binaries are stripped, and the   headers and data under `share/`; - each ELF's dynamic dependencies, SONAME, RUNPATH or RPATH, exported symbol set, and whether   it carries debug information (a `.debug_info` section) and a symbol table (a `.symtab`   section); - the unit test results, the test harness results, and the documentation examples; - the static library's global symbols, the WebAssembly module, and the Python wheel.  The tree has no git metadata. With `git` installed but no repository, the current build reports its version as `0.66+post`; built from a `git archive` export with `git` absent from `PATH`, it reports `0.66`. Paths of the build, install and scratch directories, timings, and the banner (everywhere except in the version outputs) may differ, and exported symbol names must match apart from the string lengths in template arguments (`A<n>_c`).  ## Commands that must work  Every command below must work offline, as an unprivileged user, in a fresh copy of your tree. `<N>` is the number of CPUs available (the CPU affinity, capped by the quota in `/sys/fs/cgroup/cpu.max`), `<dir>` a scratch directory.  1. Default build, unit tests and staged install:    `cmake -B build . -G Ninja -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang++`,    `cmake --build build -j<N>`, `ctest --test-dir build/tests/unit` with    `GTEST_OUTPUT=json:<dir>/` (each GoogleTest program under `tests/unit` writes its results    there; a test whose `status` is `NOTRUN` or whose `result` is `SKIPPED` need not match), and `cmake --install build --prefix /usr/local` with `DESTDIR=<dir>`. The staged    install is then placed at `/usr/local`, and the programs and `yosys-config` there are used. 2. The CI artifact, from inside `build/`: `tar -cvf build.tar share/ yosys yosys-* libyosys.so`. 3. The test job: the artifact unpacked into `build/` of a copy whose `abc` and `libs/cxxopts`    directories are empty, then `./build/yosys-config` and    `make -C tests -j<N> vanilla-test SEED=1`. The cell job: the artifact unpacked at the root of    such a copy, then `./yosys -p '<script>'` for each `test_cell` script of the `test-cells` job.    The documentation job: the artifact unpacked into `build/`, then `make -C docs test -j<N>`. 4. The test harness as it is in `/app` now, run with `make -C tests -j<N> vanilla-test` against    the products of step 1, laid out as the artifact lays them out at the tree root    (`share/`, `yosys`, `yosys-*`, `libyosys.so`). 5. Consumers of the install: `yosys-config --build plugin.so plugin.cc` and    `yosys -m ./plugin.so`; a C++ program compiled with    `clang++ $(yosys-config --cxxflags) ... -L<build> -lyosys $(yosys-config --ldlibs)` and run    with `LD_LIBRARY_PATH=<build>`; the installed `yosys-witness`, `yosys-smtbmc`, `yosys-abc` and    `yosys-filterlib`; and `yosys -Q -T -p 'help -dump-cmds-json <file>'` and    `help -dump-cells-json`. 6. Configure-only option builds,    `cmake -B <dir> -S <tree> -G Ninja -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang++ <option>`,    followed by `<dir>/yosys-config --cxxflags`, `--linkflags`, `--libs`, `--bindir` and    `--datdir`. Configuring alone must write `yosys-config` (named with the program prefix, if one    is set) into the build directory, as `make yosys-config` could without building anything.    Each option and the make variable it stands for:     | CMake option | Default | Current make variable |    |---|---|---|    | `-DYOSYS_WITHOUT_TCL=ON` | `OFF` | `ENABLE_TCL=0` |    | `-DYOSYS_WITHOUT_READLINE=ON` | `OFF` | `ENABLE_READLINE=0` |    | `-DYOSYS_WITHOUT_ZLIB=ON` | `OFF` | `ENABLE_ZLIB=0` |    | `-DYOSYS_WITHOUT_PLUGINS=ON` | `OFF` | `ENABLE_PLUGINS=0` |    | `-DYOSYS_WITHOUT_ABC=ON` | `OFF` | `ENABLE_ABC=0` |    | `-DYOSYS_DISABLE_THREADS=ON` | `OFF` | `ENABLE_THREADS=0` |    | `-DYOSYS_ENABLE_HELP_SOURCE=ON` | `OFF` | `ENABLE_HELP_SOURCE=1` |    | `-DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug` | empty | `ENABLE_DEBUG=1` |    | `-DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX=/opt/yosys` | `/usr/local` | `PREFIX=/opt/yosys` |    | `-DYOSYS_PROGRAM_PREFIX=gr-` | empty | `PROGRAM_PREFIX=gr-` |    | `-DCMAKE_CXX_STANDARD=23` | `20` | `CXXSTD=c++23` |    | `-DYOSYS_INSTALL_LIBRARY=ON` | `OFF` | `ENABLE_LIBYOSYS=1` |    | `-DBUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF` | `ON` | `ENABLE_LIBYOSYS_STATIC=1` |  7. One full alternate build and install with    `-DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX=/opt/yosys -DYOSYS_PROGRAM_PREFIX=gr- -DYOSYS_INSTALL_LIBRARY=ON -DYOSYS_WITHOUT_ABC=ON`,    then `cmake --build build -j<N>` and `cmake --install build` with `DESTDIR=<dir>`.    The static library: `-DBUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF` and `cmake --build <dir> --target libyosys`,    which must leave `<dir>/libyosys.a`. 8. The `test-compile` matrix entries this machine can run, each with `CC`, `CXX` and `CXXFLAGS`    set as the job sets them and the matrix compiler first on `PATH` as `clang`/`clang++` or    `gcc`/`g++`: clang-14 with    `CXXFLAGS=--gcc-toolchain=/usr -isystem /usr/include/c++/12 -isystem /usr/include/<triplet>/c++/12`,    where `<triplet>` is `x86_64-linux-gnu` or `aarch64-linux-gnu`, and gcc-11 with    `CXXFLAGS=-Wp,-D_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS`; then    `cmake -B build -G Ninja -DCMAKE_CXX_STANDARD=20 .` and    `cmake --build build --target yosys -j<N>`. 9. The sanitizer job, with `ASAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=1` and `UBSAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=1`:    `cmake -B build . -G Ninja -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang++ -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Sanitize -DSANITIZE=undefined,address`,    `cmake --build build -j<N>`, then `make -C tests -j<N> vanilla-test SEED=1` in the same tree. 10. The WASI job, with `/opt/wasi-sdk/bin` first on `PATH` and `<flex-prefix>/include` holding     flex's `FlexLexer.h`, as the job prepares it:     `cmake -B build -G Ninja -DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE=/opt/wasi-sdk/share/cmake/wasi-sdk-p1.cmake -DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX=/ -DYOSYS_WITHOUT_TCL=ON -DYOSYS_WITHOUT_READLINE=ON -DYOSYS_WITHOUT_PLUGINS=ON -DYOSYS_WITHOUT_ZLIB=ON -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS=-I<flex-prefix>/include \"-DCMAKE_EXE_LINKER_FLAGS=-Wl,-z,stack-size=8388608 -Wl,--stack-first -Wl,--strip-all\" .`     and `cmake --build build -j<N>`; every `build/*.wasm` must match the job's current     output. 11. The wheel, with `CC=clang`, `CXX=clang++` and pip offline:     `python3 -m pip wheel --no-index --find-links /opt/wheelhouse --no-deps -w <dir> .`; its name,     file list, metadata (including the long description), `WHEEL` and `top_level.txt`, packaged     data (`pyosys/__init__.py` and everything under `pyosys/share/`) and the ELF facts (type,     needed libraries, soname, runpath, rpath, debug sections, symbol table) of its     `pyosys/libyosys.so` and `pyosys/yosys-abc`; then the wheel     installed into a fresh venv, imported, and the tests under `tests/pyosys` run against it. 12. The vendored source package: the tree committed to a scratch repository with `.gitcommit`     reset to `$Format:%H$`, exported with `git archive`, then built with `git` absent from     `PATH`: `cmake -B build . -G Ninja -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang++` and     `cmake --build build --target yosys -j<N>`.  These commands and options must also behave like the Make build in these situations: other designs and inputs; full builds and installs at the options above (a debug build, Tcl, readline, zlib and plugins switched off, another install prefix); the installed programs and libraries used by consumers, after the installation is moved and after the build directory is gone; a binary copied out of the build directory; a build from a read-only source tree into a separate build directory; a build inside a git checkout of the tree; the WASI build without the job's linker flags; and a rebuild after files in the tree or the build directory are edited or deleted, or with nothing changed.  ## Rules  - **The Make build is gone.** Remove the top-level `Makefile`, every `Makefile.inc` outside   `abc/`, and every other piece of the Make build of Yosys and ABC; no `Makefile`, `makefile`,   `GNUmakefile` or `Makefile.conf` may remain at the root, and no `Makefile.inc` or   `Makefile.conf` outside `abc/` and `libs/cxxopts/`. No workflow, action, packaging script,   `flake.nix`, `nix/*.nix` or `Brewfile` may run `make` on the repository root, on `abc/`, or on a   makefile there. These Make uses may stay: `tests/Makefile`, `tests/common.mk`,   `tests/sva/Makefile`, `tests/unit/Makefile`, the Sphinx and code-example makefiles under `docs/`,   `examples/osu035/Makefile`, `examples/smtbmc/Makefile`, the makefiles of `libs/bigint`,   `libs/ezsat` and `libs/subcircuit`, the files inside `abc/` and `libs/cxxopts/`, workflow steps that run the test or   documentation harness with `make -C tests` or `make -C docs`, and `make` used to build   third-party sources. - **CMake builds the project itself.** The build is the `CMakeLists.txt` files and the CMake   modules they include. No `make` process may run while configuring, building or installing, ABC   included: no CMake file, and no script the build runs, may start `make`, `gmake`,   `${CMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM}` or `$(MAKE)` through `execute_process`, `add_custom_command`,   `add_custom_target` or otherwise, except to run the test or documentation harness in `tests/`   or `docs/`, and `ExternalProject` and `FetchContent` are not used. The `yosys` and `yosys-abc` executables and `libyosys` must be linked   by CMake's own rules from objects CMake compiles.
